# Implementation status

## Current integration gate: `dedafa6` outer qualification budget

The single complete cached qualifier of clean `dedafa6` terminates at its
unchanged 1,800-second outer limit: Docker records exit 124, and the wrapper
returns failure. Initial host/build checks, standard ARMv5, storage/state/owner/
loader/atomic fixtures and eight strict Samba campaigns pass: service, native,
candidate, lifecycle, fault, data, exit and held. Successful file/file-source,
the complete Samba union and the later SMART lanes are not established.

This is a failed complete run, not successful qualification assembled from
partial results. No new Samba data assertion is observed in the captured output.
The passing held guest does not
repair the earlier intermittent deadline. No genuine failed ARM capture-clock
emission is observed. Existing failure-log files predate this run and must not
be presented as its guest errors. All 1,585 tracked inputs, including 1,161 API
inputs, match their frozen witnesses after termination; the workspace is idle.
The post-success audit guard refuses this failed terminal before Docker.
No retry, budget increase, audit, PR promotion or merge follows from this result.

### Interrupted-test log retention: host-only correction

The cached wrapper now declares its existing bounded group timeout; that caller's
controller waits for its signalled foreground fixture cleanup instead of exiting
first. Other callers keep default signal handling. The Samba driver preserves
up to 1 MiB of active regular-log bytes and a 16 KiB console tail on INT/TERM,
after its existing child wait, before tmpfs deletion. Diagnostics explicitly
remain incomplete/non-qualifying; unavailable output cannot turn failure into
success. Ordinary captures and successful-run failure artifacts are unchanged.

An actual-driver regression first reproduces stale artifacts on both parent and
group SIGTERM. A real Docker PID1 timeout probe distinguishes controller teardown
from the missing cleanup copy, then preserves final synthetic stop bytes with
the same Docker exit 124. All 55 feedback tests, seven cached-preflight tests,
80 Samba fixture controls, the Windows mocked-wrapper contract and lint pass.
These compiler/image/QEMU commands are stubs: no new ARM, full integration,
source/image audit, hosted or physical qualification is claimed. No budget is
increased and the earlier failed full run remains failed. See the
[interruption contract](support/QEMU-FAST-TESTS.md#interrupted-cached-run-diagnostics).

### M4 constructor-backed pre-close input loss — focused host/ARM

The optional complete-input guest now constructs the real never-started client
Owner, normally closes one fixed owned code file and requires mismatch/review
before release. Original unattempted references and the adapter remain held;
repeated close/start/client-close calls refuse with stable reviewed FD counts,
and an independent descriptor survives. A separate helper keeps the reviewed
Owner reachable until exit; init verifies exit zero, reaping and equal bounded
parent FD counts. No client/server executes and no Owner product behavior changes.

Host checks, pinned Linux vet/selected tests, strict transcript controls and an
actual ARM guest pass at the original 60-second bound. The old three-row ARM
transcript is independently accepted by the old guard and refused by the new
five-row guard. This is pre-close containment, not kernel EIO, guaranteed FD
reuse, launched-child teardown, pending I/O or recovery. It does not replace
the failed complete run, audit/hosted gates or open product-state decisions.

A subsequently reproduced diagnostic defect lost the initial cause on repeated
review refusals. The private Owner now retains the first cause and recorded stop/
release uncertainty without accumulating a stored error chain or repeating
adapter work. Host regression/race checks and the real constructor-backed ARM
input-loss proof pass with mandatory `cause_preserved=true`. This changes only
the QEMU-only Owner, not product service activation or the full integration gate.

### M1 selected configuration protocol and anchored history

The selected direction is current coherent policy plus per-operation bundles,
not immutable generations/manifest. The experimental codec is not thereby a
frozen product format or an approved physical state layout.

An internal read-only comparison now joins multiple operation bundles to an
independently trusted exact checkpoint and current policy. It refuses duplicate
IDs, forks, gaps, changed bytes, foreign evidence and unconfirmed interior
operations; uncertain tails remain diagnostic. Count16/4MiB inspection bounds
never authorize deleting history or replacing its checkpoint. Host units/race,
strict runner controls and two actual ARM receipts across the existing API
restart pass. No extra guest or filesystem mutation is introduced by this
comparison. An explicit experimental Linux history reader now captures a bounded
complete record census and original files under the same root/policy/operations
locks. It copies an independently trusted checkpoint, binds canonical filenames
to decoded operation IDs, refuses pending metadata before content reads and
retains review after replacement/restoration. Actual standard ARM tests two
operations, the actual Store lock,16/17-record and aggregate limits, plus12
controlled close cases for the six-object two-record profile. Two exact receipts
across the original API restart and all old smoke assertions pass; no extra
default guest or enlarged timeout is introduced. Native mount-lifecycle tests
explicitly skip on the unsupported WSL kernel; those are not positive evidence.
Host/race, tagged vet and57 runner controls pass. Product-authorized publication,
provisioning/retention, recovery and product HTTP/service wiring remain required;
the latest complete integration gate above is unchanged.

A separate private QEMU/Linux mode now preserves distinct per-operation records

reads; owned writes must match the exact five directory-event publication
profile, not a watcher reset. Every admitted uncertainty fences the entire root
lifetime, even when a matching local receipt survived; a fresh ID cannot retry.

skips without unique mount IDs. Actual standard ARM verifies six fresh-state
scenarios twice: two operations/concurrent stale loser, cancellations after intent,
policy and receipt, successful policy commit followed by an injected error, and
immutable write-open. Original first record/locks, no-effect refusal and balanced
closure remain mandatory at unchanged15s/240s/360s bounds and all old smoke proofs.

Only fresh temporary namespaces are admitted. The old one-operation crash/media
tests do not qualify this new journal's failure boundaries. Its own controlled
return and syscall-success-boundary process-loss qualification follows below.
Supported filesystem restart, worst-case resources, persistent checkpoint/review
and authorized reconciliation/product HTTP/service composition remain required.
No installable firmware, product state medium or physical device qualification
is implied.

## Earlier integration failure: `0beb01e` held/data deadline

The complete cached qualifier of clean `0beb01e` passes host/race/fuzz,
standard ARMv5, storage/state/owner/loader/atomic checks and the first seven
Samba campaigns. The eighth, `held`, fails before its held-session action:
the data/runtime probe reports an execution deadline during `escape` (the
symlink-denial check). Its runtime entered with 18,077 ms remaining and reports
18,114 ms elapsed; the outer data action reports 19,998 ms budget, 1,920 ms
before the runtime probe and 20,036 ms total. These observations localize the
failure, not its cause or whether the individual worker limit is responsible.

One unchanged focused `held` guest passes strict verification with the same
guest/phase/worker/proof bounds, using the existing focused container profile.
That is not an identical full-container experiment, a reliable reproduction,
a timing repair or a replacement for the failed complete run. Offline replay
of the exact failed trace refuses it; replay of the focused trace accepts it.
The failed-terminal audit guard refuses before Docker. No further unchanged
complete retry or timeout/proof relaxation was used to select a pass.

The later file/file-source/SMART lanes and post-success audit are not reached.
Earlier focused SMART staging evidence remains separately valid. Current
complete/audit/hosted/merge, product and physical EX4 gates remain open.
PR ancestry includes its older commits, but semantic preservation still requires
diff review and exact new-head checks; it does not authorize an unstable merge.

A QEMU-only capture-return clock, subsequently published in `dedafa6`, records
the parent context around the fixed 4-second capture, before the caller's
settlement/close, without changing
generic Capture, phase budgets or failure semantics. Host controls cover the
real non-root pre-exec refusal, immutable before/after-cleanup clocks, first-error
binding, redaction and the original 1 KiB output cap; scoped race3 and whole
host checks pass. One actual ARM held guest preserves the exact healthy proof
without failure telemetry. Genuine failing ARM emission is not yet observed;
this is sharper diagnostic coverage, not causal attribution or a timing repair.

## Earlier integration failure: SMART fixture staging corrected locally

The complete cached qualifier of clean `907e172` passes host/race/fuzz, standard
ARMv5, storage/state/owner/loader/atomic checks and all ten Samba campaigns plus
their strict complete union. It then fails the synthetic SMART report guest:
the borrowed 80 MiB image has no free blocks, `debugfs` returns zero despite
allocation errors, the collector is incomplete and the init has no allocated
blocks. The kernel refuses that init with error -8. The complete run remains
failed; its later producer lane and post-success audit were not reached.

A focused unchanged ARM reproduction confirms the same failure. A real pinned
`debugfs` staging probe verifies the incomplete bytes and exhaustion. The
regression reaches QEMU before the fix and refuses before QEMU afterward.
The corrected fixture admits a base of at most 128 MiB, enlarges only its new
regular tmpfs copy to 128 MiB, and verifies each fixed injected file's exact
bytes, regular executable mode and root ownership before boot. Actual ARM
parser/collector tests and the separate synthetic producer/temperature/capture/
comparison lane pass, with the base image unchanged and original guest limits.

These focused passes do not replace a complete corrected-candidate qualifier
or establish repair of earlier Samba intermittency. No successful full/audit,
hosted/merge, product activation or physical EX4 qualification is inferred.
No persistent image, volume or device contents are enlarged by this correction.

## Independent file-service panel lifetime correction

The combined desired-policy manager now settles pending-header operations on
its original ten-second deadline or retirement, even for a non-cooperative
transport. Late results cannot publish policy, retry a save or unlock replacement
work. Its401 status recheck retains the original signal/generation: a retired
response cannot hide a replacement login or clear its policy. Both defects have
twice-reproduced deterministic RED controls; byte/structure limits, CSRF/revision
checks and uncertain-save reconciliation remain unchanged.

Whole Windows/pinned Linux suites, actual Edge against memory-only Go auth
with controlled401/status replies, and ARMv5 seven-asset HTTP/HTTPS byte parity
pass. The browser test retains original cookie-revocation and login-rate limits;
its actions are DOM-triggered, not complete pointer/keyboard/accessibility proof.
No product activation, native policy-store browser qualification, full image/
two-boot/hosted qualification or repair of the Samba gate follows.

## Passive controller: length-specific receive classification

The host-only decoder now uses the receive handler's length and fixed prefix,
not a blanket three-byte match. Thermistor sensor1/2 replies and status families
with variable payload bytes are recognized. Only MCUVer is classified at15bytes;
13-byte RTC envelopes remain unknown because their stock branch does not
validate selectors. Long button-shaped envelopes cannot change replayed button
state. The JSON shape is unchanged; `dispatch_key` remains raw bytes1..3 and
`known` means classification, not integrity, device state or execution evidence.

Three regressions fail before their respective fixes and pass afterward.
Catalog/length, raw sensor/status byte boundaries, every mixed-capture split
and CLI controls pass locally. Whole Windows and pinned Linux checks pass,
including Linux race and five fixed-count fuzz campaigns (450,000 executions).
No temperature lookup,
serial opener/transmitter, hardware test or cooling qualification is added.
Checksums and active controller support remain unqualified.

## File-service manager: bounded responses and canceled-result refusal

Six existing desired-policy method/path pairs now reuse the bounded stream
reader instead of `Response.json()`: fixed byte/structure budgets, strict UTF-8,
no Content-Length trust or unbounded fallback. Diagnostic defaults are unchanged;
policy grants/clients and combined restore get appropriately larger finite
container budgets. HTTP errors preserve status without consuming their bodies.
Canceled/failed reads release the reader without waiting for peer cancellation.
The existing operation deadline is also checked after completed body handoff;
its deterministic RED previously published current policy after cancellation.

Whole final Windows/pinned Linux checks and DOM regressions pass. Every route
has inclusive/excess controls, deadline/session retirement and handoff refusals;
uncertain PUTs remain blocked pending explicit reconciliation, never retry.

size or browser memory/performance proofs. The final single-boot ARMv5 overlay
also passes embedded-asset and existing TLS/auth smoke checks; it does not run
browser JavaScript. Other panel flows, actual browser, two-boot/full/hosted and
product qualification remain separate. No new endpoint, service activation or
device operation is added; the independent native timing gate is unchanged.

fixtures and the existing workflow path contracts pass; own hosted qualification
is separate. Module requirements, target inputs and QEMU timeouts are unchanged.

## Next state/recovery contract — proposed, not implemented

The [M1.3b recovery design](src/phantowd-api/internal/revisionstore/RECOVERY-DESIGN.md)
defines proposed bounded intent/receipt records, read-only matching, ordered work
packets and interruption/refusal tests. It distinguishes recorded local commit
confirmation from client acknowledgement and preserves current bootstrap/Owner
boundaries. Schema approval, a retained evidence collector, writer/reconciliation,
complete state-bundle provisioning and physical placement remain outstanding.
No journal, automatic recovery, HTTP surface or service activation is added by
this documentation checkpoint.

## Retained policy bootstrap authority — read-only lifetime only

`OpenBootstrapAuthorityWithCodec` and fixed NAS adapter retain the original
directory/flock, trusted codec and inotify watch until explicit verified Close.
Serialized Observe uses a fresh confined directory cursor, the existing shared
collector and original metadata/watch checks; it never reopens a parent path
or substitutes the authority's roots/backend. Other writers remain locked out
between reads. Copied/forged/serialized handles cannot read or dispose it.

Drift/read uncertainty latches instance review, including write-capable open/
close with unchanged file metadata/content and in-flight cancellation. Restored
inputs cannot resume; prework cancellation leaves the retained lifetime intact.
Explicit Close releases only this observer, with no consumers/leases. Close
errors are terminal and retain unattempted originals without retry; controlled
premature-watch-descriptor closure verifies the root/lock remains retained.
This does not qualify real kernel EIO or media cleanup failure.

Whole pinned tagged API vet/units, both store race count3, actual ARMv5 ext4
and Windows API/UI/cross checks pass on exact sources. The guest requires all52
ordered PASS receipts, strict2+133 controls, normal unmount and unchanged base;
no prior observer/interruption/media assertion or timer is removed. No new
Docker image/volume/default CI boot/privilege or NAS operation.

No init/sync/publish, lease, Owner handoff/admission, journal/recovery/HTTP or
product startup is added. A valid current value is not durable confirmation;
empty directory is not NAS pristine. Review is not durable across restart and
closing/reopening must not become automatic recovery. Next: state-bundle and
durable intent/receipt/reconciliation contracts before admission; physical
placement/hardware/full/hosted/reproduction/installer gates remain.

## Policy bootstrap observation — read-only values, not admission

`revisionstore.ObserveBootstrapWithCodec` and fixed-name/schema
`naspolicystore.ObserveBootstrap` inspect protected policy state without fsync,
initialization, pending promotion/deletion or publication. A temporary exclusive
directory flock, O_NOATIME, existing no-follow/current same-filesystem fence,
metadata brackets and an empty private inotify epoch protect the observation.
Only kind/revision values return, not policy content/names/descriptors or leases.

| Outcome | What it observes, not authorizes |
| --- | --- |
| `empty-directory` | Complete bounded empty scan; not whole-NAS pristine or enrollment permission |
| `valid-current-policy` | Stable bounded current document passes codec; positive revision, not acknowledged/durable publication |
| `pending-evidence` | Safe pending metadata; bytes are never decoded/promoted, even with current present |
| `invalid-or-unsupported-policy` | Stable readable document fails codec; no reset or separate migration/version claim |
| `unrecognized-entries` | Unknown/additional evidence; no usable revision or complete unknown-file inventory claim |

Busy/unavailable/unsafe/canceled/drifting reads return error/zero, never empty
state. Actual write-capable open/close during decode refuses even with unchanged
data/metadata. No Store/Owner admission, HTTP/startup or durable review changes;
the shared codec validator is equivalent to the previous constructor checks.

Whole pinned tagged Linux vet/units, both store-package race count3 and Windows
API/UI/cross checks pass. Two strict ARM drivers pass, but their aggregate raw
capture was truncated. A subsequent same-source guest independently supplies
all47 ordered PASS receipts, normal unmount and unchanged base. Strict2+123
controls and all prior interruption/full/read-only assertions remain. No new
Docker storage/default CI boot/NAS/privilege or timer change. This is not the
product state bundle, retained admission, durable recovery, schema migration,
physical placement, full/hosted/reproduction or installation qualification.

## Original client handle observations — composed ARMv5 prerequisite

An opt-in test build of the fixed client now calls the pinned source reader on
the same original context/file after OPEN/stat and again immediately before
CLOSE. It copies and validates session/tree/persistent/volatile IDs, emits bounded
canonical notices and requires fieldwise equality; drift or uncertain observation
refuses without CLOSE, reopen or retry. This variant accepts only `C`, has no
write/seek imports and does not enable pending writes. The ordinary client is
unchanged in behavior and its mock/ARM compile regression passes.

ASan/UBSan orchestration mocks verify exact ordered notices, 64-bit values,
sixteen observer/identity/auth refusals, forbidden controls and existing operation
failures. The new ARMv5 executable links a fixed observer SONAME, never its scratch
path. The standalone reader guest still passes its real IDR/36-refusal seam and
three loaded-DSO early refusals; positive connection getters remain mocked.
The optional `pending-handle` campaign now executes this client against the real
native Samba server in a disposable ARMv5 guest. Independent inspection qualifies
the fixed observer closure; shared dependencies must match the original client
closure exactly. The containing Owner retains all original code/loader/module/
document descriptors and fixes the adapter mode once at construction. Its bounded
parser accepts only ordered canonical notices, caches the complete initial tuple
and refuses changed or replayed observations. Raw IDs do not enter public logs.

The actual client observes its original mapping twice. The containing composition
matches its session/tree IDs to the independent STATUS witness and rechecks the
original process, namespaces, reciprocal TCP connection, server opening and local
object before CLOSE. Verified client exit/group absence and daemon settlement
precede descriptor release; unchanged file bytes and final FD/base equality pass.
The previous `pending` guest also passes as a regression. The default ten guests,
generic launcher and original deadlines are unchanged. Run
`support/test-samba-root.ps1 -Campaign pending-handle` for this separate proof.

Two previously hidden assumptions were corrected without relaxing admission:
the linker emitted RPATH, not the previously documented RUNPATH; explicit new
dtags and exact readelf guards now require RUNPATH. Samba's actual `DLIST_ADD`
sets a singleton's `prev` to itself, not NULL; a source-built RED/GREEN control
now uses that macro and the reader requires the real singleton representation.
Ten sensitive source hashes pin both allocation/list definitions as well as the
mapping/getters. The separate standalone positive getter seam remains mocked.

This is not the missing remote-FileId-to-server-file-generation bridge. Client
persistent/volatile IDs are **not** STATUS `share_file_id`. Complete fault,
request/extent/cache/journal and WRITE qualification remain open;
`smb_object_bound=false` and `pending_io=false` remain mandatory. No HTTP, NAS,
current complete-image or hosted timing qualification is added. See the
[focused contract](support/QEMU-FAST-TESTS.md#remaining-original-handle-witness).

The positive seam mocks connection/session/tree getters. Separately loading the
actual DSO exercises three early invalid-context refusals, not healthy SMB.
Two valid/nineteen invalid receipt controls, byte-exact insertion, unchanged
borrowed base and Windows API/UI/vet/ARM cross checks pass. Partial-link GC fixes
unused upstream wire/event DSO references without weakening undefined-symbol
checks; fixed private-library RUNPATH fixes the observed loader refusal.

Run [the source-reader lane](support/QEMU-FAST-TESTS.md#remaining-original-handle-witness).
It creates no persistent image/volume and changes no default campaign, product
launcher or proof flag. Original-client retained integration, an independent
same-server-open bridge, request/extent/cache/journal, WRITE, full-image/hosted
and physical-device qualification remain open. A successful copied observation
is not a lifetime lease or execution authority. Firmware is not installable.

## Native timing diagnosis: focused host/QEMU only

Current QEMU-only orchestration now keeps the original live revocation and
session-continuity checks inside 45 seconds, then allows a separate 15 seconds
for fresh-login denial and peer authentication. The combined maximum explicitly
changes from 45 to 60 seconds; this is not a performance optimization. The SAME
retained authority crosses the seam. Failed, canceled or expired first work
never continues or retries, and exact monotonic deadline checks reject late
success even before asynchronous context cancellation becomes visible.
Actual-runner virtual-clock RED/GREEN verifies the accounting boundary and
deadline fence. Pinned Go 1.26.8 tagged command vet/tests, phase race-count3,
ARMv5 test cross-compilation and Windows API/UI preflight pass. The actual native
ARMv5 campaign also passes on the ordinary two-CPU local profile, including data,
stop/reap and descriptor proofs; independent replay refuses five altered or
incomplete records. A separate 0.6-CPU stress run completes continuity in 44.235
seconds and fresh authentication in 3.894 seconds, then closes authority, but
hits the unchanged 180-second guest limit before the data/final proofs. That run
fails; partial phase success is not complete guest qualification or a proven
data-path deadlock. Complete-image/default-union/own-head hosted qualification
remain pending. Worker/readiness/stop/guest180 and product limits remain
unchanged. First-phase expiry still fails; no old CI gate is waived.

A current-source local control now reproduces the hosted **fresh-login / shared
45-second deadline** pattern: only the disposable container's CPU quota changes
from 2 to 0.6 after client preparation. Revocation and changed-pair checks finish;
fresh login starts with about 3.1 seconds left and refuses at 45.05 seconds. The
complete log independently fails the unchanged strict verifier. This demonstrates
a CPU-quota-sensitive wall-time path, not the actual hosted cause or a fix.

A temporary ARMv5 parent-process CPU profile, removed after capture, covers the
post-preparation campaign including closure/data verification. SHA-256 and system
calls dominate sampled cost; cumulative percentages overlap, and preemption
samples do not prove overhead. It does not measure subprocess CPU or the live
interval alone. Original source is restored byte-exact. No cache, timer, retry,
preemption or authority change follows; hosted qualification remains required.

Test-only read-schedule controls now compare the real 32 KiB digest helper with
32/64/128/256 KiB controls, including differential boundary/length tests. An
isolated ARMv5 guest passes those regressions and all five lanes in three
alternating-order rounds; every measured operation checks digest and count.
Time drift prevents a causal service-speedup claim. A separate pinned compiler
PGO control shows no compelling digest advantage. Neither optimization is
adopted; production hashing, cancellation and all service deadlines remain.

The host campaign-refusal path now reports bounded redacted worker timing row
counts/stage sums plus the last observation and last reported failure, before
the unchanged synthetic log tail. Missing/unsupported/malformed observations
are unavailable, never acceptance. Reporting failure preserves campaign exit1;
the original verifier, contexts, deadlines and Go/guest code are unchanged.
Windows77 tests (five skips), pinned Linux77 tests/lint/shell checks and actual
ARM failure-log replay pass, including the real shell failure block and fallback.
Row sums are not wall time, operation counts, coverage or root-cause proof.
An earlier reduced-CPU control fails during enrollment, not the hosted
fresh-login stage. A one-variable test-only async-preemption-off control also
fails enrollment; its temporary change is removed with all1321 inputs restored.
This does not qualify that flag as a fix or exclude every scheduling contributor.
No new full guest/image/hosted qualification or service activation follows.

Fixed QEMU-only live-operation and worker-stage telemetry now separates the
shared phase budget from individual admission/execution/settlement checks.
Typed labels, bounded durations and redacted cause classes disclose no account,
command, password or worker output; diagnostic rows never qualify a campaign.
Original contexts, verification calls, deadlines and no-retry behavior remain.
Pinned Go 1.26.8 vet/unit/race tests and ARMv5 cross-compilation pass.

One focused native ARMv5 campaign passes with its mandatory proofs and an
independent log replay: the live phase finishes in approximately 30 of 45 seconds.
An unchanged-source CPU-quota control exhausts the same 45-second parent during
the changed-pair post-admission check, after revocation completes. An earlier

These are distinct measured boundaries, not a causal fix for intermittent timing.

## Roadmap comparison

M5.5a local correction on `fab3b7e` (complete tree `60e04f9`) closes a
deterministically reproduced browser-state race: the existing snapshot could
render retired diagnostic values after a completed logout. The four reads now
share one single-flight generation/controller and a finite 10-second deadline
through body decoding. Authentication boundaries abort/clear that generation;
late successful/error/auth-body responses and finalizers cannot alter a newer
session. Eleven actual-source DOM cases across five groups pass, including
logout/global logout/password change, auth failure, timed-out body, duplicate
reads and a newer pending snapshot. The complete UI suite passes repeatedly;
its success marker now follows every test, not just an earlier subset.
Windows API/UI/vet/tagged/cross-compilation, pinned Linux whole API tagged
vet/race and existing feedback/fuzz contracts pass. Actual ARMv5 standard smoke
and clean two-boot overlay pass with the current embedded assets; seven base
hashes independently remain unchanged. These fixtures do not run JavaScript
in a real browser and do not qualify accessibility, target installation or
product authentication. No HTTP authorization, endpoint, privileged operation
or storage behavior changes. One existing image/output and bounded disposable
RAM scratch reused; no second heavy PR. M5/overall planning bands unchanged.
The correction rebases as `b8e8814` onto qualified PR #76 with both original
patches unchanged, API subtree `2871d3b1` and the same dashboard test blob.
The complete expected composition uses the proven tree-equivalent original
PR75 head as its explicit squash base; actual rebased tree equals `2bffe030`.
Repeated current-source Windows API/UI/vet/tagged tests and ARMv5 test
cross-compilation pass. This is not a new QEMU execution or real-browser proof.

(complete tree `3055351`), joins a validated desired policy to the existing
immutable inventory through two internal transient interface bindings. Private
overlapping counts report exact/missing static prefixes, same-address/peer use
and subnet overlaps across the full local inventory, blocked link/address flags,

unbound slots. Unsupported bindings and invalid/canceled inputs return zero

Windows API/UI/vet/tagged tests and ARMv5 cross-compilation pass. Pinned Linux
whole API tagged vet/race, fixed 2,000-case mutation/order tests, existing bounded
wire/route/object fuzz campaigns, 7 kernel/21 feedback tests and real workflow

One existing image/output and read-only workspace are reused, with bounded

The separate native boundary follow-up adds eight actual-producer tests per
release for checksum policy and power-query errors/default continuation.
Both 7.4/7.5 suites and the existing seven report projections/vet/race pass

Percentages are **engineering planning estimates**, not measured test coverage,
probabilities of success, release readiness, or a delivery-date promise. Credit
includes source, negative tests and fixture integration; the remaining scope
includes product wiring, operator recovery and hardware qualification. Bands
remain wide because physical storage, cooling and installation may require
substantial redesign. Rows overlap and must not be added.

| Milestone | Implemented evidence | Main remaining acceptance work | Estimated completion |
| --- | --- | --- | ---: |
| M0 — Engineering baseline | Pinned builds, signatures/hashes, host/race/fuzz tests, QEMU, SBOM; an earlier independent reproduction | Reliable hosted integration, unresolved fixture failures, renewed release reproducibility | 50–65% |
| M1 — Durable state | Revision stores, intent/result journals, refusal of uncertain state, synthetic clean-reboot tests | Product state placement, bootstrap/schema migration, operator reconciliation, real durability/power-loss qualification | 30–45% |
| M2 — Identities | Unix allocation/creation; Owner-bound Samba disabled-first enrollment, explicit enable/disable and account-session revocation in QEMU | Complete ownership/import inventory, product boot authority, account API/UI, retirement and recovery | 50–65% |
| M3 — Storage lifecycle | Complete sysfs census, generation-bound read-only broker, GPT/ext/MD observations, collision checks, scoped desired-volume review, internal mount/lease fixtures | Persistent logical VolumeID resolver, global-use accounting, production qualifier/roster, supported layouts and EX4 media qualification | 40–55% |
| M4 — SMB/NFS | Real loopback clients, desired policies, coherent candidate planner, process-set supervision, share-scoped handoff, grant-only isolated runtime and retained static-code Owner with explicit supervised lifecycle in QEMU | Approved daemon runtime manifests, isolated process sets, privilege profiles/ACLs, transactional activation/recovery, production wiring and storage-loss monitoring | 35–50% |
| M5 — Management UI/security | Development authentication/TLS, sessions/password changes, diagnostics dashboard and policy preview/editing | Product enrollment/reset/certificate lifecycle, authorized live workflows, recovery UX, browser/accessibility/security qualification | 25–40% |
| M6 — Network/system | Dual-stack desired-policy model, private bounded read-only kernel inventories and internal local-address diagnostics in host/ARMv5 QEMU; brief two-port board research | Qualified interface/external-conflict and routing admission, safe network transactions/rollback, supported dual-port modes, time/discovery, notifications and administrative jobs | 5–15% |
| M7 — Board/cooling/recovery | DTS and bounded diskless RAM trials; passive MCU framing/catalog tooling | Qualified factory identities, fan/tach/fail-safe, LCD/LED/buttons/power/watchdog, SATA/USB and NAND recovery | 15–25% |
| M8 — RAID/health/migration | Generic offline GPT/ext/MD inspectors, static WD layout analysis and synthetic offline SMART report interpretation | Attributable EX4 layout corpus/importer, ownership/ACL migration, RAID jobs, trusted SMART collection/history/jobs/UI, backup/restore tests | 15–25% |
| M9 — iSCSI | Specification and legacy research; no product target implementation | ARMv5 backend selection, LUN model, session/ownership guard, migration and failure campaigns | 0–5% |
| M10 — Installer/upgrades | Host-only signed release/payload/version verification | Reviewed boot/state/install layout, target installer, signing-key lifecycle, boot health/rollback and demonstrated unbrick | 10–20% |
| M11 — Public release | Portions of test infrastructure, package SBOM and earlier reproducibility evidence | Measured EX4 budgets/mixed load, independent security/legal review, physical recovery campaign and signed release assets | 5–15% |

Overall planning band: **roughly 25–40% of the first-release engineering scope**.
This is a judgment weighted toward storage/sharing, board safety and migration,
not an average of file counts. **0 of 12 milestones are product-qualified**;
there is no installable beta. Applications/mobile/multi-model support are
excluded. No calendar estimate is defensible before the hardware/recovery gates.

## Evidence map and integration gaps

M0.3 local build-environment follow-up: two successful exact-head hosted jobs
(PR77 `37136391769`, PR78 `37140806223`) took 3410/3465 seconds. In the PR78
log, host-cmake bootstrap/configure/build consumed about 11 minutes before
host-Go, despite an exact compiler-cache hit: this dependency cannot use
ccache. The pinned container now supplies snapshot-authenticated system CMake
3.25.1, accepted by the current QEMU configuration's Buildroot minimum 3.18.
The source-contract regression first fails with CMake absent; 23 Linux
feedback tests and shell/workflow contracts then pass. A fresh disposable
configuration proves ordinary host-tool selection with no host-cmake cache
dependency and refusal of an unsuitable version. An actual cold host-ccache
4.10.2/dependency build passes in 86 seconds using only existing source archives
and disposable tmpfs, without network or host-cmake compilation. Frozen
`4202bbf` then passes the complete local cached Buildroot lane: pinned native
vet/unit/race/fuzz, target/legal/SBOM, regular-image probe, ARMv5 smoke, MD,
two-boot state, isolated runtime/Owner/loader, atomic, Samba/ACL and SMART
fixtures. Seven generated artifact hashes independently match after terminal
exit zero; the builder is removed and only the two fixed volumes/one pinned
image tag remain. This is not measured hosted speedup, clean independent
reproducibility, EX4 or a product milestone. No target feature, compiler-cache
trust rule or guest assertion is weakened; no new persistent workspace or
device operation is added. That initial complete local run used integrated
`d1b8dfe`, before PR79. After guarded PR79 integration, rebase `46341c7`
retains all four qualified CMake input blobs and the exact PR79 API subtree.
Whole tagged Linux vet/race, repeated SIGKILL regressions, feedback contracts
and actual ARMv5 standard/two-boot overlay pass for this union. Seven base
artifacts remain unchanged; the overlay does not regenerate legal/SBOM or
qualify a new clean image. The build-environment follow-up's own hosted
acceptance remains pending.

- M0: [build harness](support/container/build-qemu.sh),
  [QEMU workflow](.github/workflows/qemu-armv5.yml),
  [fast-lane limitations](support/QEMU-FAST-TESTS.md).
- M1/M2: [administrator store](src/phantowd-api/admincredentials/README.md),
  [service store](src/phantowd-api/fileservicestore/README.md),
  [identity Owner](src/phantowd-api/identityowner/README.md) and
  [credential lifecycle](src/phantowd-api/SMB-CREDENTIAL-LIFECYCLE.md).
- M3/M4: [read-only helper](src/phantowd-volume-probe/README.md),
  [mount Owner](src/phantowd-api/internal/mountowner/README.md),
  [process Owner](src/phantowd-api/internal/processowner/README.md) and
  [candidate planner](src/phantowd-api/internal/fileserviceplan/README.md).
  The mount qualification/driver remains QEMU-only. The fixed roster is not
  an appliance discovery provider. The handoff's original volume path remains
  reachable in the host mount namespace; a read-only clone alone is not an
  authorization boundary. A separate single-static-child isolated runtime now
  closes that bypass in its disposable grant-only fixture, not for ordinary
  process sets or real daemons. Ordinary API startup activates neither runtime.
- M5/M6: [management component](src/phantowd-api/README.md) and
  [startup code](src/phantowd-api/main.go). Saving desired policy is not applying it.
- M7: [board research](board/wd/ex4/README.md) and
  [MCU tooling](tools/phantowd-lab/mcuproto/). Protocol selectors and sensor
  observations do not establish a functioning, safe hardware-control daemon.
- M8/M9: [compatibility matrix](STORAGE-COMPATIBILITY.md) and
  [offline inspectors](tools/phantowd-lab/diskimage/). All legacy migration
  layouts remain unqualified; metadata consistency is not health or mount authority.
  [SMART report interpretation](src/phantowd-api/internal/smartreport/README.md)
  is offline only; no report provenance, freshness or device control is established.
- M10/M11: [host verifier](tools/phantowd-lab/releaseverify/) and
  [GitHub release reader](tools/phantowd-lab/githubrelease/). Neither installs firmware.

## Validation and CI findings

The local wrapper's opt-in `-CachedOnly` mode encodes the previously passing
bounded local profile without building/pulling an image, explicitly creating
volumes or repairing ownership. It refuses absent cache inputs and mismatched
current-config output before compilation. Mock command-boundary tests cover
the fixed arguments and seven refusal paths; seven real Linux shell-preflight
tests cover initialized, missing, changed-config and nonwritable inputs.
The actual complete wrapper run subsequently passed on unchanged `9a40538`
(tree `60ddde8d`), including all host/race/fuzz/image/legal/SBOM and guest lanes.
Seven artifacts independently match the preceding baseline; the temporary
container is removed, with only the two fixed volumes, one 14 GiB output and
approximately 971 MiB compiler cache remaining. Exact-head
[host workflow](https://github.com/PhantoNull/phantowd-ex4/actions/runs/37094418091)
also passes, including PowerShell and the Linux preflight. This qualifies this
cached local wrapper, not independent reproduction or physical/product safety.

The unmodified audited baseline passed the local pinned incremental Buildroot
pipeline on 2026-10-02: Linux vet/unit/race and bounded fuzz, native volume-probe
fixtures, standard ARMv5 QEMU smoke, mdadm-authored MD v1.0 comparison and the
two-boot state fixture. Existing fixed cache/workspace volumes were reused.
This is not an independent clean build or EX4 qualification.

Hosted evidence is separate:

- [Run 36990449160](https://github.com/PhantoNull/phantowd-ex4/actions/runs/36990449160)
  compiled successfully but hit the standard smoke's 120-second readiness
  deadline. Its detailed failed-guest artifact is now retained. A local
  CPU-limited differential reproduced timeout at 121 seconds and completed
  the same full smoke at 162 seconds with the new bounded 240-second budget.
  This supports budget exhaustion; it does not conclusively rule out another
  hosted problem. No assertion is removed and QEMU is not retried. This run
  never reached the separate MD/state fixtures, so it does not confirm the
  executable-mode correction below. Later exact-head validation passed in
  PR #54 and PR #55; this does not retrospectively validate the failed run.
- [Run 36967317155](https://github.com/PhantoNull/phantowd-ex4/actions/runs/36967317155)
  failed Linux process lifecycle tests after the expensive build. Later
  handshake/timing test changes and current local race checks pass; they do not
  retrospectively validate that failed revision.
- [Run 36975422468](https://github.com/PhantoNull/phantowd-ex4/actions/runs/36975422468)
  passed standard guest smoke and failed the MD v1.0 fixture. The workflow did
  not upload its saved failure log. The current baseline passes locally; the
  hosted fixture failure's precise root cause remains unresolved.
  Follow-up differential QEMU testing found a concrete clean-checkout defect:
  the MD fixture's PID-1 script was recorded as Git mode `100644`, while Docker
  Desktop local builds installed it executable. Setting only the copied image's
  init inode to `0644` reproduced kernel `EACCES`/panic; `0755` passed the complete
  MD/Owner fixture. The executable Git-mode correction and an early source-mode
  regression now address this discrepancy. The old run's missing detailed log
  prevents proving that no additional defect was involved. PR #55 passed the complete
  exact-head hosted QEMU lane, including the MD fixture.
- Current workflow concurrency separates PR and branch refs: a merged PR can
  continue compiling alongside its `develop` integration. Cancel only a
  confirmed superseded run, not a still-needed qualification run.
- A compiler-cache miss followed by downstream failure previously prevented
  saving the completed compilation, causing the next run to rebuild cold.
  Compiler-cache reuse must never imply test success or release qualification.

The feedback improvement runs pinned Linux tests before the full target build,
prints the last 120 guest-log lines on failure, uploads failure-only diagnostics,
and permits only non-cancelled trusted `develop` pushes with a fresh completed-
compile checkpoint to seed the bounded compiler cache. It does not suppress
tests, retry mutations, or claim the historical MD/state failures are fixed.
The exact cache key was absent on the latest failed run. Restore now tries
older QEMU/Linux compiler-cache entries only after exact-input keys; ccache
still revalidates compilation inputs and the trusted-only write/size rules
are unchanged. This is not a cached workspace or an established speedup.

## Next implementation sequence

The integrated PR #55 implements a native
static-ELF launcher and internal fixed-input `IsolatedOwner`, passing disposable
ARMv5 namespace/root/FD/privilege/signal/PID/read-only tests, seven refused
launches, real readiness/stop, and pre-launch/live input-loss quarantine. See its
[contract](src/phantowd-service-launcher/README.md). It is not connected to
product startup or ordinary process Set and does not complete M4.4 or change
the planning bands above. Runtime manifests, product root construction, leases, native
daemon integration and kernel NFS authority remain unresolved.

Published follow-up `aecfdb4` passed the full **local incremental** pinned
Buildroot pipeline on 2026-10-02: Linux vet/unit/race/fuzz, target/legal/SBOM,
native regular-image probe, standard ARMv5 smoke, MD v1.0 comparison, separate
two-boot state fixture and native/Go isolated Owner fixture. All seven artifact
hashes were rechecked. The standalone launcher and its Go fixture are injected
only into a temporary test image, not installed by the firmware package.
This does not qualify a clean build, hosted integration, physical EX4 or
Samba/kernel-NFS activation. PR #54 and PR #55 later passed their exact-head
hosted QEMU checks; those are separate evidence, not release approval.

1. **M0.3:** validate the feedback changes locally and in exact-head CI; retain
   failed-guest evidence. Investigate any recurrence before changing semantics.
2. **M4.4:** integrate the independently tested launcher into one trusted,
   fixed process boundary per service, preserving supervised PID/
   process-group ownership; private mount namespace with private propagation
   and restricted root. Pin only the granted share roots and necessary runtime
   inputs; remove access to original volume paths, inherited host root/FDs,
   devices and mount privileges. Probe actual Linux/ARMv5 behavior before
   choosing a production mechanism. No HTTP path or product enablement.
3. **M3/M4:** complete trusted production roster/qualification and durable
   logical volume identities; then a single service configuration/activation
   owner with freshness, leases, readiness, stop-before-release and uncertain-
   outcome recovery. Do not copy QEMU-only constructors into product startup.
4. **M1/M2/M5:** product-owned persistent authority/startup and operator
   reconciliation, then authorized account/share management workflows.
5. **M7/M8/M10:** separately reviewed exact-model hardware, cooling, healthy
   disposable-media migration and recovery evidence before installation.

## Re-audit: execution path, backlog and next deliverable

The ordinary API entrypoint (`src/phantowd-api/main.go`) wires diagnostics,
authentication, desired policy and manual read-only GPT observations. It does
not instantiate the mount/process/identity Owners as a persistent product
activation manager. `package/phantowd-api/S50phantowd-api` likewise starts only
the non-root development API. The isolated launcher remains a fixture-injected
helper, not an installed package. This is the main difference between our many
tested building blocks and a usable appliance, not a missing cosmetic screen.

The earlier statement above that the launcher has no handoff/runtime
composition is superseded by `f7571ea` **only for a dedicated grant-only handoff
and one static non-root child**. Ordinary process sets, multi-user Samba and
kernel NFS control retain their separate gaps. The feature is already pushed;
at this audit it has seven commits beyond integrated `develop`, zero committed
changes waiting for push, and PR #54 is the only open PR. Do not manufacture
extra PRs or merge a still-pending exact-head check to reduce the count.

The host-only `inspect-runtime-closure` now reuses the existing ELF inventory
instead of a second parser. Pinned Linux Go 1.26.6 vet/unit/race checks and
50,000 fuzz executions pass. On the existing Buildroot target, the restricted
candidate for `usr/sbin/smbd` resolves **105 distinct regular ELF objects,
27,110,832 bytes**. A regression distinguishes an entirely empty RUNPATH
(ignored by glibc) from unsafe empty components of a nonempty search list.
Windows API/UI and lab-tool preflights also pass. This is an offline extracted
view, not a fresh image build, ARMv5 loader comparison or permission/ABI/runtime
qualification. All candidate outputs explicitly deny execution authority.

Continue M4.4 with these concrete acceptance steps:

1. **Local differential complete:** actual ARMv5 loader in a disposable QEMU
   image resolves 104 dependencies, exactly matching the 105-object candidate
   excluding the main executable. Guest hashes and canonical aliases match;
   both default and 1000:1000 builders pass, original rootfs unchanged. This does
   not start Samba or qualify its runtime/privileges. Seven host refusal/budget
   tests, lint and existing feedback/workflow contracts pass. Full-build hooks
   now reuse this comparison; the complete local incremental pipeline passed
   on unchanged `357be1d`: Linux vet/unit/race/fuzz, package/license/SBOM checks,
   standard ARMv5 smoke, MD v1.0 comparison, two-boot state, native isolation and
   loader match. Seven generated-artifact hashes passed. This reused existing
   caches, not an independent clean build, hosted run or hardware qualification.
2. Inventory Samba's required `dlopen`/NSS modules, configuration, state, sockets
   and privilege transitions. Keep root/UID switching distinct from the generic
   zero-capability child; do not widen that helper or use shared client identity.
3. Construct the trusted per-service root from independently pinned, validated
   runtime inputs and explicit share grants; prove denied original paths,
   ungranted shares and read-only bypass attempts, then stop-before-release.
4. Integrate a service-specific owner and recovery transaction only after the
   complete production storage qualifier/roster and state authority exist.

Hosted update: PR #54 head `d827026` passed both host (`37001276886`) and
ARMv5/DTB (`37001276464`) checks and merged into `develop` as `3902f97`.
The successful hosted smoke took about 124 seconds under the new 240-second
bound; the old 120-second budget would not cover it. The roughly 84-minute job
spent most time compiling. Older compiler cache restored, but its speedup is
not measured. The normal `develop` run is separate from this exact-head result.
Historical timeout, executable-mode and state-unmount failures remain distinct;
this pass does not resolve intermittent EBUSY or qualify physical hardware.
No new image or named volume was created for the dependency research.

The separate [Samba restricted-root fixture](support/SAMBA-RUNTIME-PROFILE.md)
now passes locally with real distinct-user SMB3 access, owner/mode checks,
kernel read-only enforcement, original-path/symlink denial, one UTF-8 filename
and process-group stop. It retains only six root capabilities for Samba identity
switching; the generic non-root helper is unchanged. This is disposable ext4
and a cached image overlay, not an Owner-backed product root, daemon activation,
full dynamic-module/encoding/ACL qualification. The whole updated incremental
pipeline also passed on unchanged `fba213d`, including Linux race/fuzz,
package/license/SBOM, standard ARMv5, MD, two-boot state, native Owner/handoff,
loader and this Samba experiment. This is not hosted feature CI or an
independent clean build; existing fixed caches were reused.

Host/QEMU work can continue now. Valuable disks, the live NAS and NAND/MTD
remain outside this implementation/test scope.
